# evaluation/ -- the counters behind the numbers of record

The scripts and small inputs that computed the evaluation's numbers of record (`paper/numbers-of-record.md` in the project's documentation repository), gathered by the analysis lane that produced them (relays 151 §2 and 152). They are instruments, not part of the tool: nothing under `evaluation/` is built by the workspace or shipped in the submission source zip.

**Numbers of record:** the landed head `32d966b9f` (60s'), census `batch60sp` (cache-only on the solve cache `era5c-r215-kindorder`), environment of record `crates/pointer_replacer/frames/l01p14u-s39-mutlend-o-env.sh`.

## Layout and how to run

- **The layout mirrors the relative roots the scripts use.** `evaluation/scratch/analysis-fanout/<path>` is a file of the lane's scratch tree, at its path there; `evaluation/docs/<path>` a file of the documentation repository. A script's relative default (`scratch/analysis-fanout/r143/reconcile.json`, `docs/agents/tools/...`) therefore resolves to the copy here when the script is run from `evaluation/`. The counters are indexed by family in the table below (`inator-types`, `positions-uses-decl`, `census`, `rq5`, `conf`, `waivers`, `sites-and-timing`, `record-tooling`, `extra` = producers a completeness pass added).
- **Inputs that are not copied** (the census outputs, the solve caches, this repository, the paper, CROWN's artifact) are reached through the same relative roots: from `evaluation/`, link `scratch/main` to the census output directory (e.g. its `batch60sp/`), `worktrees/` to the directory of solve caches (`worktrees/era5c-r215-kindorder/`), `crat` to this repository (`ln -s .. crat`), `paper/` to the paper repository, `crown-artifact/` to CROWN's artifact, `home/.local/opt/` to the z3 install, `tmp/scratchpad/` to a scratch work directory. The two Laertes container scripts take the image's artifact directory as `LAERTES_ARTIFACT` (the `artifact/` directory in the home of user reviewer, in `aliasing-limits-oopsla23:final`).
- **Hosts:** `host-a` is the measuring host (AMD Ryzen 9 9950X, 16 cores / 32 threads, 128 GiB); `host-b` the second host (the same CPU model), where the solves and &inator ran.
- **Rust instruments** (`rq2_pos_counter`, `rq2_decl_driver`, the conversion counter of report 114, `deref-tool`, `release-tool`): each crate carries its `rust-toolchain.toml`; build it in its own directory with that toolchain (`cargo build --release`). Their `Cargo.lock` files are not copied (registry URLs) -- their sha256 are under *Not copied*. They are not workspace members.

**The rewrite** (the only change against the files as run; per-file counts in `FILES.tsv`): the user's home-directory prefix becomes a relative root by subtree (the lane scratch tree -> `scratch/`, the documentation repository -> `docs/`, the paper repository -> `paper/`, this repository -> `crat/`, the worktree directory -> `worktrees/`, CROWN's artifact -> `crown-artifact/`, the rest of the development directory -> `dev/`, the rest of the home -> `home/`); a temporary scratchpad path -> `tmp/scratchpad/`; the Laertes image's artifact path -> `${LAERTES_ARTIFACT}`; the two host names -> `host-a` / `host-b`; the user's login and name -> `user`; the c2rust fork's owner names -> `c2rust-fork` / `fork` / `fork-owner`; assistant, vendor and review-tool names -> `assistant` / `vendor` / `reviewer-tool`; a mail domain -> `mail`; a colleague's name -> `colleague`; the code host's domain -> `git-host`; a co-author trailer word -> `co-written`. **308 files, 1546 rewrites.**

| extra | seed_g5.py | producer of rq4.suite.coverage.delivered-functions | rq4.suite.coverage.delivered-functions | analysis-fanout 088 | its own header (verbatim): analysis-fanout relay 108, group g5 (rq3 / rq4 / setup keys): seed what is measurable at a frame. usage: seed_g5.py <census-dir> <solve-dir> [--out <tsv>] [--largest brotli] [--rounds-programs p,q,...] READ ONLY. No build, no cargo / rustc / miri, no solver. Cache entries are never loaded: the rounds are read with mmap + bytes.find over the entry (the page cache only; nothing parsed but small integers). Keys written (key \t value \t provenance): rq3.largest.check-s, rq3.rest.check-s the census's `<p>.raw-boundary-timings.tsv` column `initial_verify_s`: the wall of the FIRST compile check of the emitted crate inside the census worker (bo_rewriter/mod.rs:2440-2470 at 4a47f16f7: `verify::diagnose_crate` = an in-process rustc session running `tcx.analysis(())`, i.e. parse + expansion + type check + borrow check, no codegen, panic=abort config). For a program whose verify loop ran more than one round the later rounds' checks are NOT recorded (only `atom_reverify_s`, 0 everywhere at L01^14); the value is the first round's. largest = the named program (default brotli = setup.largest-name); rest = sum over the others. rq3.largest.rounds the solve's cache entry for the largest program (workers/<p>.json completed.cache_entry): per demand-evidence construction (one per verification), the number of `final_selections` (one per selector solve = one per validation round, construction.rs:1215/1427, solver.rs:929-949); the value is the larger of the verifications (analysis-fanout 067/068's definition of `analysis.rounds.per-program`). Cross-check: the sum over constructions equals the count of `"round":` records in the `retirement-rounds` family. rq3.inator.single-trial-min the protocol constant of rq3-inator relay 004 §1 ("five trials where a trial is under ten minutes, one stated trial otherwise"), read from the docs repo origin/main. setup.host, setup.host-ours, setup.host-inator, setup.rustc, setup.solver /proc + /etc/os-release (this host); the solve's worker receipts' `host`; rq3-inator report 001 §3 (host-b); rust-toolchain.toml at the census's code frame + the installed toolchain's channel manifest; the solve env's z3 library path + its wheel METADATA, Cargo.lock's z3 crates. setup.depth.third-level-slots the solve's cache entries' `model` map (bounded head reader, r100/keymap/model_head.py): slots whose key ends `@dK` with K >= 2, and their kinds (all twenty programs). rq4.suite.coverage.delivered-functions wave-6o's coverage.py `delivered()` definition verbatim (docs/agents/tools/runtime/coverage.py:106-112): distinct `owner_fn` of `<p>.raw-boundary-subject-outcomes.tsv` rows with delivery=realized-as-predicted and emitted_form != unchanged, summed over the six shipped-suite programs (R817-1). | python3 / bash on its arguments as its header states | `docs/agents/artifacts/2026-10-07-analysis-fanout-088/stage/p4-086rows/g5/seed_g5.py` e7006f359883f28fce9f29295c3cf2740b21d4c2781f8317a946e5afba6dcc64 |

| extra | constructs.py | producer of rq2.constructs.* | rq2.constructs.* | analysis-fanout 086 | its own header (verbatim): analysis-fanout r108 g4-rq2: rq2.constructs.<construct>.<tool> (Appendix E, Table tab:rq2-constructs). usage: constructs.py <census dir> [--out <dir>] The table's caption: "Per-construct coverage: analyzed, declined, or unsupported. Where a system's stance is documented in its own paper it is cited at the cell; where the disclosure cannot be produced symmetrically, the affected comparison carries the asymmetry and no superiority claim." Each cell is one word. Definitions used here (stated so the cells can be audited): analyzed - the system's analysis reasons about pointers that flow through the construct, and the construct's pointers can come out in a safe form; declined - the system recognizes the construct and keeps its pointers raw by design (out of its scope), without failing on the input; unsupported - the system has no handling: its paper says it does not support the construct, generates no constraints for it, may crash on it, or the inputs had to be modified to remove it. Third-party cells are frame-independent: read from the tools' own papers (paper-workspace/related/A_direct-comparison, text extracted with pdftext/pdftxt.py in this directory; quotes in STANCES) and, for streams where a paper is silent, from the outputs CROWN's artifact distributes (benchmarks/rs-crown-transformed; crown-artifact/ comparison) -- never a Laertes run of ours (R858-3). Our cells carry the code cite at the frame and an evidence count measured on <census dir>, so the script re-runs at a new frame. Writes <out>/constructs.tsv (key, value, provenance). | python3 / bash on its arguments as its header states | `docs/agents/artifacts/2026-10-07-analysis-fanout-086/g4-rq2/constructs.py` 6cbeaf9e300a60de9acb9707d61cfd23cf12288e1f576cf8323ef786d7842309 |
